0.55489pt\raise-0.83192pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces}}}},

respectively. The second condition that

\mathbf{GNS}
be an oplax-natural transformation
is that the compositions in these
two diagrams are equal.
This follows from the commutativity of the
individual squares and triangles in the diagram

(3.32)

\textstyle{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime\prime}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{A}}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{A}}}/\mathcal{N}_{\omega\circ f\circ f^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{A}}}/\mathcal{N}_{\omega\circ f}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{A}}}/\mathcal{N}_{\omega}}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{f\circ f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{L_{f^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{L_{f}}
\scriptstyle{L_{f\circ f^{\prime}}}

for any state
\omega
on
{{\mathcal{A}}}.

By continuity, this equality extends to the completions.
∎

Physics 3.33
.

Oplax-naturality means the following
if we restrict our attention
to a subalgebra and then restrict to yet another
subalgebra, as in

(3.34)

{{\mathcal{A}}}_{1}\xhookrightarrow{j}{{\mathcal{A}}}_{0}\xhookrightarrow{i}{{\mathcal{A}}}.

Equality of the two diagrams in
(
3.31
)
means that constructing the
physical subspace
{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega\circ i\circ j}

of
{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega}

of quantum configurations
for the state
\omega
with respect to the subalgebra

{{\mathcal{A}}}_{1}
is the same subspace obtained
from first restricting to
{{\mathcal{A}}}_{0}
and then
to
{{\mathcal{A}}}_{1},
i.e.

(3.35)

\textstyle{{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega\circ i\circ j}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega\circ i}}
\scriptstyle{L_{i\circ j}}
\scriptstyle{L_{j}}
\scriptstyle{L_{i}}

commutes, where we have used the notation from
Construction
3.17
.

Remark 3.36
.

\mathbf{GNS}
being an oplax-natural transformation
provides the correct categorical structured needed to reflect the
functoriality of the GNS construction as can be seen
by the equality of the diagrams in
(
3.31
).
In current terminology
[
11
]
, Theorem
3.26
shows
that the GNS construction is not only
a functor for a fixed
C^{*}
-algebra
{{\mathcal{A}}},
but it is also a morphism
of pre-stacks over the category of all
C^{*}
-algebras. Note that it is
not

a morphism of pre-sheaves of categories because
the outer diagram in (
3.24
) does

not
commute (a condition that
is required to have a morphism of
pre-sheaves). Instead, a natural transformation
(which is a 2-morphism in
\mathbf{Cat}
)
is needed to compensate for the lack of commutativity,
and this is why 2-categories play a crucial role in the GNS construction.

4.
Some comments on the category of states

One would like to think of
\mathbf{States}({{\mathcal{A}}})
as a category
of states with non-trivial morphisms. Namely,
a morphism from
\omega:{{\mathcal{A}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathbb{C}}}
to
\mu:{{\mathcal{A}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathbb{C}}}

consists of a
C^{*}
-algebra morphism

\phi:{{\mathcal{A}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{A}}}
such that the diagram

(4.1)

\textstyle{{{\mathcal{A}}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{A}}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathbb{C}}}}
\scriptstyle{\omega}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\scriptstyle{\mu}

commutes, i.e.
\mu\circ\phi=\omega.

Let us call this closely related category

\mathcalligra{states}\;({{\mathcal{A}}}).

While one can define a functor

\mathcalligra{states}\;({{\mathcal{A}}})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbf{Rep}({{\mathcal{A}}})

as a special case
of
\mathbf{GNS}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}
on objects
and
\mathbf{GNS}_{\phi}
on morphisms,
this is too restrictive and not
what we want in general because
mappings of
different
algebras show up in many applications.
Recall from Physics
2.15
, that
a
C^{*}
-algebra map

{{\mathcal{A}}}_{0}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{A}}}
is
supposed to be thought of as
using macrostate observables
described by
{{\mathcal{A}}}_{0}

instead of microstate observables
described by
{{\mathcal{A}}}.

To incorporate this, we would therefore
still want to think of the different categories
of states as a pre-sheaf of categories on
the category of
C^{*}
-algebras, i.e. a functor

\mathcalligra{states}:\mathbf{C^{*}\text{-}Alg}^{\mathrm{op}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbf{Cat}.

For a morphism
f:{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{A}}}
this should
get mapped to a functor

\mathcalligra{states}\;(f):\mathcalligra{states}\;({{\mathcal{A}}})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathcalligra{states}\;({{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}).

How should this functor be defined? This
agrees with
\mathbf{States}(f)
at the level of objects.
However, for a morphism
\phi:\omega\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mu

of states in
{{\mathcal{A}}},
all we have is the
collection of morphisms

(4.2)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 68.15279pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-68.15279pt\raise 45.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern From these data, we are supposed to produce
a map of
C^{*}
-algebras
\phi^{\prime}:{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}
such that
the diagram

(4.3)

\textstyle{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{{{\mathbb{C}}}}
\scriptstyle{\omega\circ f}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\mu\circ f}

commutes. One can show that the only such
maps
f:{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{A}}}
of
C^{*}
-algebras
for which we can do this in a functorial
manner are
C^{*}
-algebra

isomorphisms
. Since we specifically
do not want this for physical reasons, we
use the discrete category
\mathbf{States}({{\mathcal{A}}})

instead of the more reasonable, yet naive,
category
\mathcalligra{states}\;({{\mathcal{A}}}).

5.
A right adjoint to the GNS construction

Besides producing a representation

(\pi_{\omega},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega})
of
{{\mathcal{A}}}

given a state
\omega
on
{{\mathcal{A}}},
the GNS construction
also produces a cyclic vector in
{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega}.
This fact
will let us construct a sort of
inverse to the GNS construction
provided that we include this extra datum in the
definition of the oplax-natural transformation

\mathbf{GNS}.

Definition 5.1
.

A
cyclic vector

\Omega

for a representation
\pi

of a
C^{*}
-algebra
{{\mathcal{A}}}
on a Hilbert space
{{\mathcal{H}}}

is a normalized (i.e. unit) vector
\Omega\in{{\mathcal{H}}}
such that

(5.2)

\{\pi(a)\Omega\;:\;a\in{{\mathcal{A}}}\}

is a dense subset in
{{\mathcal{H}}}
(with respect
to the norm induced by the inner product
on
{{\mathcal{H}}}
).
A representation
(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}})
of
{{\mathcal{A}}}

together with a cyclic vector
\Omega
is called a

cyclic representation

and is written as a triple
(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega).

A representation
(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}})
of
{{\mathcal{A}}}

together with a normalized vector (not necessarily cyclic)
is called a
pointed representation
.

The reason for demanding normalized vectors in the above definition
is so that they produce states, as will be explained shortly.

Physics 5.3
.

When
{{\mathcal{A}}}
is the algebra of observables
for a quantum field theory (in a particular phase), the vacuum vector
is typically a cyclic vector—any particle content
state is obtained by creation operators on the ground state.
When a representation is irreducible, every
non-zero vector is cyclic—by using annihilation operators,
one can get to the ground state.
One should take this comment with a grain of salt due
to domain and distribution issues (see Folland
[
12
]
).
A finite-dimensional and completely rigorous example occurs
in the theory of spin by use of ladder operators.

Definition 5.4
.

Let
\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}})
be the category
of pointed representations of
{{\mathcal{A}}}.

Namely, an object of

\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}})
is a pointed representation
(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)
of

{{\mathcal{A}}}
and a morphism from
(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)
to
(\pi^{\prime},{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime})

is an intertwiner
L:{{\mathcal{H}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\prime}
of
representations of
{{\mathcal{A}}}
such that

(5.5)

L(\Omega)=\Omega^{\prime}\qquad\&\qquad L^{*}L=\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{H}}}}.

Let
\mathbf{Rep}^{\odot}({{\mathcal{A}}})

be the sub-category of
\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}})

of cyclic representations of
{{\mathcal{A}}}.

Proof.

Some things must be checked so that the above
definition is in fact valid. For instance, let

(5.6)

(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)\xrightarrow{L}(\pi^{\prime},{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime})\xrightarrow{L^{\prime}}(\pi^{\prime\prime},{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\prime\prime},\Omega^{\prime\prime})

be a pair of composable morphisms. Then the
composition
L^{\prime}L
satisfies

(5.7)

(L^{\prime}L)^{*}(L^{\prime}L)=L^{*}L^{\prime*}L^{\prime}L=L^{*}L=\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{H}}}}.

Associativity follows from associativity of
composition of functions. The other
axioms of a category all hold.
∎

Remark 5.8
.

\mathbf{Rep}^{\odot}({{\mathcal{A}}})

is a full subcategory of
\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}})

because a vector being cyclic is a property
and not additional structure.

The following lemma will be useful for proving that certain
linear maps are isometries.

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
{{\mathcal{H}}}
and
{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\prime}
be Hilbert spaces and let
L:{{\mathcal{H}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\prime}

be a bounded linear map. The following conditions on
L
are equivalent.

(a)

L^{*}L=\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{H}}}}.

(b)

\lVert L\psi\rVert^{\prime}=\lVert\psi\rVert
for all
\psi\in{{\mathcal{H}}}.

(c)

\langle L\psi,L\phi\rangle^{\prime}=\langle\psi,\phi\rangle
for all
\psi,\phi\in{{\mathcal{H}}}.

In this notation, a prime superscript has been used to distinguish
the norm and inner product on
{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\prime}
from that of
{{\mathcal{H}}}.

Remark 5.10
.

The condition
L^{*}L=\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{H}}}}
implies that
L
is injective.
We do
not
require
L
to be unitary,
which would impose
LL^{*}=\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\prime}}
as well.
However, note that if
L:(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(\pi^{\prime},{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime})

is a morphism of
cyclic
representations, then

L
sends a dense subset of
{{\mathcal{H}}}
to a
dense subset of
{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\prime}
because
L(\Omega)=\Omega^{\prime}.

Therefore, in this case,
L
is unitary.
Also note that the number of morphisms from a cyclic representation
to a pointed representation is quite small: there is either
one or none at all.

Construction 5.11
.

Let
(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)
be a pointed representation
of a
C^{*}
-algebra
{{\mathcal{A}}}.
The vector
\Omega

defines a state
\omega_{\Omega}
on
{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{H}}})
by
the formula

(5.12)

{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{H}}})\ni B\mapsto\omega_{\Omega}(B):=\langle\Omega,B\Omega\rangle.

We often refer to
\omega_{\Omega}
as a vector state.
Pulling this state back to
{{\mathcal{A}}}
along
\pi
defines a
state
\omega_{\Omega}\circ\pi:{{\mathcal{A}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathbb{C}}}
on
{{\mathcal{A}}}.

\omega_{\Omega}\circ\pi
is sometimes denoted by

\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\big((\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)\big)
for “restriction.”

Remark 5.13
.

If the vector
\Omega
were not required to be normalized, but merely
nonzero, then
one could still define a state by the assignment

(5.14)

{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{H}}})\ni B\mapsto\frac{\langle\Omega,B\Omega\rangle}{\langle\Omega,\Omega\rangle}.

Nevertheless, such an assignment would spoil other
desirable properties that will be discussed shortly
(see Remark
5.54
).

Lemma 5.15
.

Let
L:(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(\pi^{\prime},{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime})

be a morphism of pointed representations
of
{{\mathcal{A}}}.

Then,

(5.16)

\omega_{\Omega^{\prime}}\circ\pi^{\prime}=\omega_{\Omega}\circ\pi,

i.e. the two states

\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\big((\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)\big)

and

\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\big((\pi^{\prime},{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime})\big)

are equal.

Proof.

For any
a\in{{\mathcal{A}}},

(5.17)

\begin{split}\left\langle\Omega^{\prime},\pi^{\prime}(a)\Omega^{\prime}\right\rangle&=\langle L(\Omega),\pi^{\prime}(a)L(\Omega)\rangle\qquad\text{ by (\ref{defn:pointedRepobjects})}\\
&=\langle L(\Omega),L\pi(a)\Omega\rangle\qquad\text{ by (\ref{eq:intertwiner})}\\
&=\langle\Omega,L^{*}L\pi(a)\Omega\rangle\qquad\text{ by Def'n of $L^{*}$}\\
&=\langle\Omega,\pi(a)\Omega\rangle\qquad\text{ by (\ref{defn:pointedRepobjects})}.\end{split}

∎

Physics 5.18
.

Imagine a context in which
we begin with a representation of
{{\mathcal{A}}}
on a Hilbert space
{{\mathcal{H}}}

and a vacuum vector
\Omega\in{{\mathcal{H}}}.
Given
a subalgebra
{{\mathcal{A}}}
of
{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{H}}}),
the
construction
\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}
restricts the
vacuum state
\omega_{\Omega}:=\langle\Omega,\;\cdot\;\Omega\rangle
on
{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{H}}})

to a state on this subalgebra.
This is useful if we can only make
measurements of certain observables.
For instance, a Rindler observer has a restricted
algebra of observables so that restricting
a Minkowski vacuum state to their
algebra results in a thermal state, a
phenomenon known as the Unruh effect
[
10
]
.
If we change our representation
in such a way
that the two are still related by an intertwiner
satisfying (
5.5
),
then we get the
same
state.
Note that it is not required that
L
be unitary—an isometry
preserving the unit vector suffices.

\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}
is also a natural construction from
the physics perspective since every
unit vector in
{{\mathcal{H}}}
gives a state on
any
C^{*}
-subalgebra of
{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{H}}}).

What is not obvious is that there is
a canonical way to go back—the purpose of this
section is to make this statement precise
and prove that the GNS construction
achieves this.

Proposition 5.19
.

Let
{{\mathcal{A}}}
be a
C^{*}
-algebra.
The assignment

(5.20)

\begin{split}\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}})_{0}\ni(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)&\mapsto\omega_{\Omega}\circ\pi\in\mathbf{States}({{\mathcal{A}}})_{0}\\
\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}})_{1}\ni\Big((\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)\xrightarrow{L}(\pi^{\prime},{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime})\Big)&\mapsto\mathrm{id}_{\omega_{\Omega}\circ\pi}\in\mathbf{States}({{\mathcal{A}}})_{1}\end{split}

from Construction
5.11

defines a functor

\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}:\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbf{States}({{\mathcal{A}}}).

Proof.

This follows directly
from Construction
5.11

and Lemma
5.15
.
∎

Construction 5.21
.

Let
{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{f}{{\mathcal{A}}}
be a morphism
of
C^{*}
-algebras. The induced functor

\mathbf{Rep}(f):\mathbf{Rep}({{\mathcal{A}}})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbf{Rep}({{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime})
extends
to a functor

\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}(f):\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime})
as follows.
Let
(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)
be a
pointed representation of
{{\mathcal{A}}}.
Then
this gets sent to
(\pi\circ f,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega).

Note that even if
(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)
is a cyclic
representation,

(\pi\circ f,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)
is
not
necessarily a cyclic
representation of
{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}
since

(5.22)

\left\{\pi\big(f(a^{\prime})\big)\Omega\;:\;a^{\prime}\in{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}\right\}

is not necessarily dense in
{{\mathcal{H}}}.

Nevertheless,
(\pi\circ f,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)

is

a pointed representation.
A morphism of pointed representations
of
{{\mathcal{A}}}
gets sent to a morphism of
pointed representations of
{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}

under the functor
\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}(f)

using the same intertwiner. In fact,
the diagram

(5.23)

\textstyle{\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{States}({{\mathcal{A}}})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{States}({{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}(f)}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{States}(f)}

commutes.

This proves the following fact.

Proposition 5.24
.

\mathbf{rest},
as defined in Construction

5.11
,
is a natural transformation
21
21

21

This is special case of a
pseudo-natural transformation
since
\mathbf{rest}_{f}=\mathrm{id}
in
(
5.23
).

(5.25)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 84.51105pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-84.51105pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{C^{*}\text{-}Alg}^{\mathrm{op}}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 47.81604pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-3.43056pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{Cat}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.11928pt\raise 23.4014pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.4014pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{States}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 48.10547pt\raise 6.42934pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-13.03716pt\raise-24.42209pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.22223pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rest}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 15.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}.

Physics 5.26
.

Following the earlier examples of
a subalgebra
{{\mathcal{A}}}_{0}\hookrightarrow{{\mathcal{A}}},

one interpretation of the functor

\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}}_{0}\hookrightarrow{{\mathcal{A}}}):\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}}_{0})
can
be given as follows.
Let
(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)
be a cyclic representation of
{{\mathcal{A}}}

with
\Omega
viewed as a vacuum vector for some quantum field theory.
View
{{\mathcal{A}}}_{0}
as a subalgebra of
{{\mathcal{A}}}
corresponding
to low energy observables. Low energy observables
may be unable to produce states of the parent theory whose rest mass
is beyond the energy scale of the low energy observer.
This corresponds to the fact that the vector
\Omega
might no
longer be cyclic with respect to the pullback representation
to the algebra
{{\mathcal{A}}}_{0}
(cf. Physics
5.3
).

Commutativity of the diagram (
5.23
),
and hence naturality of
\mathbf{rest}
, says that the state the low
energy observer sees is independent of the order in which they
disregard certain information. The observer can either
forget the representation first and then focus on the
low energy observables or first focus on the induced low energy
representation and then forget the representation.
The induced states are the same.

We will now modify the GNS construction to
include the construction of a cyclic vector.
Due to the similarity of this construction
and that of Constructions
3.1

and
3.17
,
we will skip many details and only focus on
the new ones.

Construction 5.27
.

For every
C^{*}
-algebra
{{\mathcal{A}}},
define a functor

(5.28)

\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}:\mathbf{States}({{\mathcal{A}}})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}})

by the following assignment. To a state

\omega:{{\mathcal{A}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathbb{C}}},
assign
the cyclic representation
22
22

22

[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}]
is a cyclic vector
because
\{\pi_{\omega}(a)[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}]=[a]\;:\;a\in{{\mathcal{A}}}\}=:{{\mathcal{A}}}/\mathcal{N}_{\omega}

is dense in
{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega}
by definition.

\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}(\omega):=(\pi_{\omega},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega},[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}]).

Because
\mathbf{States}({{\mathcal{A}}})
has no non-trivial morphisms,
this defines a functor.
Furthermore, the image of this functor actually lands
in the subcategory

\mathbf{Rep}^{\odot}({{\mathcal{A}}})

[
2
]
.
To every morphism
{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{f}{{\mathcal{A}}}

of unital
C^{*}
-algebras,
define a natural transformation

(5.29)

\textstyle{\mathbf{States}({{\mathcal{A}}})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{States}({{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{States}(f)}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}(f)}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}_{f}}

as follows. To every state
\omega:{{\mathcal{A}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathbb{C}}}
on

{{\mathcal{A}}}
define the morphism

(5.30)

\big(\pi_{\omega\circ f},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega\circ f},[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}]\big)\xrightarrow{\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}_{f}(\omega)}\big(\pi_{\omega}\circ f,{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega},[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}]\big)

of pointed representations
to be exactly the same as
L_{f}

in (
3.19
)
and simply note that a property of this linear
map is that

(5.31)

L_{f}([1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}])=[f(1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}})]=[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}]

since
f
is a morphism of
unital

C^{*}
-algebras.

L_{f}^{*}L_{f}=\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega\circ f}}

follows from the calculation (
3.21
)
and Lemma
5.9
.

Remark 5.32
.

Note that although

\big(\pi_{\omega},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega},[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}]\big)
and

\big(\pi_{\omega\circ f},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega\circ f},[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}]\big)

are cyclic representations of
{{\mathcal{A}}}
and
{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime},

respectively,
the pointed representation

\big(\pi_{\omega}\circ f,{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega},[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}]\big)

of
{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}

obtained by pullback along
f
is
not

necessarily cyclic. This is why the target of
the GNS functor was chosen to be
the category of pointed representations instead
of cyclic representations. This is analogous
to the fact that the restriction of an irreducible
representation to a subalgebra need not be
irreducible.

Theorem 5.33
.

The assignments

(5.34)

\begin{split}\mathbf{C^{*}\text{-}Alg}^{\mathrm{op}}_{0}&\xrightarrow{\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}}\mathbf{Cat}_{1}\\
{{\mathcal{A}}}&\mapsto\Big(\mathbf{States}({{\mathcal{A}}})\xrightarrow{\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}}\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}})\Big)\end{split}

and

(5.35)

\begin{split}\mathbf{C^{*}\text{-}Alg}^{\mathrm{op}}_{1}&\xrightarrow{\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}}\mathbf{Cat}_{2}\\
\Big({{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{f}{{\mathcal{A}}}\Big)&\mapsto\Big(\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}\circ\mathbf{States}(f)\xRightarrow{\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}_{f}}\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}(f)\circ\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\Big)\end{split}

from Construction
5.27

define an oplax-natural transformation

(5.36)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 84.51105pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-84.51105pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern Proof.

The proof is not much different than what

\mathbf{GNS}
in Theorem

.
∎

Physics 5.37
.

The weak form of naturality for
\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}
guarantees
that the way in which the Hilbert spaces and their cyclic vectors
fit into the larger space is independent of how the restrictions to
successive subalgebras is grouped. For instance, one could
imagine a sequence of course-grained subalgebras. This naturality
on one pair of subalgebras guarantees consistency for all such
tuples of subalgebras when applying the GNS construction.

There is one last construction we must
confront. This involves relating
the composition of
oplax-natural transformations

\mathbf{rest}
and
\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}

with the identity natural transformation.

Lemma 5.38
.

The vertical composition

(5.39)

\textstyle{\mathbf{C^{*}\text{-}Alg}^{\mathrm{op}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Cat}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{States}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{States}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rest}}

of oplax-natural transformations is equal to the identity natural transformation.

Proof.

Let
{{\mathcal{A}}}
be a
C^{*}
-algebra.
The composition acting on a state
\omega:{{\mathcal{A}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathbb{C}}}

gives

(5.40)

\begin{split}&\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 116.00409pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-116.00409pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{States}({{\mathcal{A}}})}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-23.07983pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}})}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 63.99591pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{States}({{\mathcal{A}}})}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-58.01308pt\raise 6.24155pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.24155pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-23.07983pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.29324pt\raise 5.72223pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.72223pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 63.99591pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}
}}}}\\
&\qquad\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 81.11226pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-81.11226pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\omega}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-16.7976pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big(\pi_{\omega},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega},[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}]\big)}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 74.15372pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}],\pi_{\omega}(\ \cdot\ )[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}]\big\rangle_{\omega}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-68.88773pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-16.7976pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.7976pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 74.15372pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}
}}}}.\end{split}

\big\langle[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}],\pi_{\omega}(\ \cdot\ )[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}]\big\rangle_{\omega}

agrees with
\omega
as a state on
{{\mathcal{A}}}
because

(5.41)

\big\langle[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}],\pi_{\omega}(a)[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}]\big\rangle_{\omega}=\big\langle[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}],[a]\big\rangle_{\omega}=\omega(1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}^{*}a)=\omega(a)\quad\forall\;a\in{{\mathcal{A}}}.

There are no non-trivial morphisms
in
\mathbf{States}({{\mathcal{A}}})
so the composition is the identity
functor.
To every morphism
f:{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{A}}}
of
C^{*}
-algebras,
the composition of natural transformations

(5.42)

\textstyle{\mathbf{States}({{\mathcal{A}}})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{States}({{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{States}({{\mathcal{A}}})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{States}({{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{States}(f)}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}(f)}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{States}(f)}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}=\mathbf{rest}_{f}}

must equal the identity natural transformation.
This follows immediately from the fact that the compositions
on the top and bottom of this diagram are
identities by (
5.41
)
and since
\mathbf{States}({{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime})
has no non-trivial morphisms.
∎

Physics 5.43
.

The interpretation of this is immediate when viewing
\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}
and

\mathbf{rest}
as processes/constructions. If you start with a state,
construct the GNS representation, and then forget the representation,
you get back your original state. In other words, there is no loss
of information.

However, the composition in the order

(5.44)

\textstyle{\mathbf{C^{*}\text{-}Alg}^{\mathrm{op}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Cat}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{States}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rest}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}}

is certainly not the identity. In the following,
we construct the required modification
(see Definition
A.10

in the Appendix).

Construction 5.45
.

Let
{{\mathcal{A}}}
be a unital
C^{*}
-algebra
and consider the diagram

(5.46)

\textstyle{\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{States}({{\mathcal{A}}})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}})}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}_{\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}})}}

of functors.
Recalling the notation from Constructions

5.11
and
3.1
,
observe what happens to a pointed
representation

(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)
of
{{\mathcal{A}}}
along the top two functors

(5.47)

\textstyle{(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)}
\textstyle{\big\langle\Omega,\pi(\ \cdot\ )\Omega\big\rangle=:\omega}
\textstyle{\big(\pi_{\omega},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega},[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}]\big)}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}}

Therefore, we have two pointed representations of
{{\mathcal{A}}}

whose associated states agree, i.e.

(5.48)

\langle\Omega,\pi(a)\Omega\rangle=\big\langle[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}],\pi_{\omega}(a)[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}]\big\rangle_{\omega}\quad\mbox{ for all }a\in{{\mathcal{A}}}.

If

(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)
were
also
a cyclic
representation, then
it was already known by Segal
that any other cyclic representation
restricting to the same state is unitarily
equivalent to it
[
2
]
.
We slightly modify Segal’s proof for our construction.
Define the linear map

(5.49)

\begin{split}{{\mathcal{A}}}/\mathcal{N}_{\omega}&\xrightarrow{m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\big((\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)\big)}{{\mathcal{H}}}\\

Note that if

Remark

shows that

Remark 5.54
.

If, in the definition of a pointed representation, we use
arbitrary vectors instead of normalized ones and we define

\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)
to be the state

(5.55)

\frac{\langle\Omega,\pi(\ \cdot\ )\Omega\rangle}{\langle\Omega,\Omega\rangle},

then the map (
5.49
) cannot be an isometry
unless
\langle\Omega,\Omega\rangle=1.

Physics 5.56
.

The map
m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\big((\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)\big)
tells us
that if we start with an arbitrary representation

(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}})
of the algebra of observables
{{\mathcal{A}}}

together with a normalized
vector
\Omega\in{{\mathcal{H}}}
(the representation
need not need be irreducible and the vector
need not be cyclic), if we forget about our
Hilbert space, and remember only the algebra
of observables and our state, then we might not
be able to recover our exact Hilbert space back,
but we can get close. The best we can do from the
GNS construction is to get a new Hilbert space
that embeds into the Hilbert space we started with
via
m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\big((\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)\big).

Furthermore, in this subspace, the vector
we started with becomes cyclic with respect to the
algebra of observables.
In other words, we lose some information,
namely the vectors orthogonal to this subspace,
but we keep many of the essential features of our
initial state and our algebra of observables.

0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{{}{}{}{{{}}{{}}{}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}}{}{}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-7.60193pt\raise-28.03287pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.6014pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-17.2804pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}

i.e. for every object
(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)

of
\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}})
with

\omega:=\langle\Omega,\pi(\;\cdot\;)\Omega\rangle,

the diagram

(5.61)

\textstyle{\big(\pi_{\omega\circ f},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega\circ f},[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}]\big)}
\textstyle{\big(\pi_{\omega}\circ f,{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega},[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}]\big)}
\textstyle{(\pi\circ f,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}_{f}(\omega)}
\scriptstyle{\;\;f^{*}\left(m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\big((\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)\big)\right)}
\scriptstyle{m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}\big((\pi\circ f,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)\big)}

of intertwiners of pointed representations of

{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}
must commute.
The image of a vector

[a^{\prime}]\in{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}/\mathcal{N}_{\omega\circ f}
under the top
two linear maps is
\pi\big(f(a^{\prime})\big)\Omega
while
the image under the bottom map is

(\pi\circ f)(a^{\prime})\Omega.
These are equal
elements in
{{\mathcal{H}}}.
Because the maps
agree on a dense subspace, the diagram
(
5.61
) commutes.
Finally, when
m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}
is restricted to
the subcategory

\mathbf{Rep}^{\odot}({{\mathcal{A}}}),

it was shown at the end of Construction
(
5.45
)
that it is unitary on objects of
\mathbf{Rep}^{\odot}({{\mathcal{A}}})

and therefore a vertically invertible
natural transformation.
∎

Physics 5.62
.

Commutativity of (
5.61
), i.e.
m
being a modification,
encodes the fact that the way in which our GNS Hilbert spaces
sit inside the original space agrees with respect to the smaller
collection of observables in the case that one is restricting
to a subalgebra.

Everything we have done up to this point
leads to the following theorem
encompassing the GNS construction.
To state it, we introduce the functor 2-category
(see Definition
A.28
).

Definition 5.63
.

Let
\mathbf{Fun}(\mathbf{C^{*}\text{-}Alg}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathbf{Cat})

be the 2-category whose objects are
functors from
\mathbf{C^{*}\text{-}Alg}^{\mathrm{op}}
to
\mathbf{Cat},

1-morphisms are oplax-natural
transformations, and 2-morphisms
are modifications. Compositions and identities
are defined as in ordinary 2-category theory
(see Appendix
A
for definitions).

Theorem 5.64
.

The oplax-natural
transformation

\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}:\mathbf{States}\Rightarrow\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}

is left adjoint to
\mathbf{rest}.
In fact,
the quadruple
(\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet},\mathbf{rest},\mathrm{id},m)

is an adjunction in

\mathbf{Fun}(\mathbf{C^{*}\text{-}Alg}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathbf{Cat}).

Proof.

The only thing left to check are the
zig-zag identities from Lemma

A.29
.
Using the notation from that lemma,

F:=\mathbf{States},

G:=\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet},

\sigma:=\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet},

\rho:=\mathbf{rest},

\eta:=\mathrm{id},

and

\epsilon:=m.

By Remark
A.32
,
it suffices to prove

(5.65)

Fortunately,
these identities are essentially tautologous.
For (
5.65
), since

\mathbf{States}({{\mathcal{A}}})
has no non-trivial morphisms,
the equality holds. For (
5.66
),
it suffices to check what happens to a state
\omega.

Under the composition in (
5.66
),

\omega
gets sent to

(5.67)

\omega\mapsto\big(\pi_{\omega},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega},[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}]\big)\mapsto\big\langle[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}],\pi_{\omega}(\ \cdot\ )[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}]\big\rangle=\omega\mapsto\big(\pi_{\omega},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega},[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}]\big),

which is exactly the same representation as
in the second step. Finally,

m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\big((\pi_{\omega},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega},[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}])\big)

is the identity intertwiner because it sends
[a]\in{{\mathcal{A}}}/\mathcal{N}_{\omega}

to
\pi(a)[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}]
by (
5.49
) but
\pi(a)[1_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}]=[a]

by (
3.13
).
∎

In particular, by Remark
A.32
,
this theorem implies the following.

Corollary 5.68
.

For every
C^{*}
-algebra
{{\mathcal{A}}},
the quadruple
\big(\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}},\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}},\mathrm{id},m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\big)
is an adjunction (in the usual sense).

This means that for every state
\omega\in\mathbf{States}({{\mathcal{A}}})_{0}
and pointed representation

(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)\in\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}})_{0},
there is a natural bijection of morphisms
24
24

24

This is how one remembers that
\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}
is
left
adjoint
to
\mathbf{rest}.

(5.69)

\hskip-5.69054pt\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}})\Big(\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}(\omega),(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)\Big)\cong\mathbf{States}({{\mathcal{A}}})\Big(\omega,\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)\Big),

which illustrates
in what sense the GNS
construction
\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}(\omega)
is optimal:
for every other choice of representation

(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)
on which to realize the state
\omega
as the restriction of a
vector state, there is always a (unique)
isometric intertwiner from the GNS Hilbert
space to
{{\mathcal{H}}}.
In particular,
the GNS Hilbert space is the

smallest
space on which one can
represent a state as the restriction of a vector state.
If
\omega
and
\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\big((\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)\big)
do not
agree on
{{\mathcal{A}}},
this result also says that there is
no
intertwiner
between the pointed representations

\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}(\omega)
and
(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)

(since
\mathbf{States}({{\mathcal{A}}})
is a discrete category).
This can change if we have an
isomorphism of our algebra.

A special case of the adjunction

\big(\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}},\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}},\mathrm{id},m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\big)

occurs when restricted
to the category of cyclic representations
of
{{\mathcal{A}}}.
In this case,
it is an adjoint equivalence (meaning, an
equivalence of categories).
In other words, in the cyclic case,
the categories
\mathbf{Rep}^{\odot}({{\mathcal{A}}})

and
\mathbf{States}({{\mathcal{A}}})
are equivalent
and the restriction functor exhibits this equivalence
with a canonical inverse given by the
GNS construction. In particular,
this reproduces the well-known result
[
2
]
that
there is a one-to-one correspondence between
isomorphism classes of cyclic representations
of
{{\mathcal{A}}}
and states on
{{\mathcal{A}}}.

Our results can be summarized
by saying that we can now provide
a
definition

instead of a
construction

that produces, in a functorial manner,
cyclic representations from states
on
C^{*}
-algebras.

Definition 5.70
.

The
GNS construction

is the left adjoint of
\mathbf{rest}.

This means that the GNS construction is characterized by
the following data:

-algebra
{{\mathcal{A}}}

subject to the following conditions:

(a)

{{\mathcal{G}}}_{f}(\omega)
is an isometric intertwiner
of representations of
{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}
from

{{\mathcal{G}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}(\omega\circ f)
to
25
25

25

Here,
f^{*}
is the pullback of a representation
along the map
f.

f^{*}{{\mathcal{G}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}(\omega)

for all states
\omega\in{{\mathcal{S}}}({{\mathcal{A}}}),

(b)

m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\big((\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)\big)
is a isometric intertwiner
from
{{\mathcal{G}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\Big(\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\big((\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)\big)\Big)

to
(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)
for all pointed representations

(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)\in\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}})_{0},

(c)

{{\mathcal{G}}}_{\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}}=i_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\circ{{\mathcal{G}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}},

where
i_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}:\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}})_{0}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}})_{1}
is the
map that assigns the identity intertwiner to each pointed
representation,

(d)

{{\mathcal{G}}}_{f\circ f^{\prime}}(\omega)=f^{\prime*}\big({{\mathcal{G}}}_{f}(\omega)\big)\circ{{\mathcal{G}}}_{f^{\prime}}(\omega\circ f)

for all composable pairs of
C^{*}
-algebra morphisms

{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{f^{\prime}}{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{f}{{\mathcal{A}}}
and states

\omega\in{{\mathcal{S}}}({{\mathcal{A}}}),

(e)

f^{*}m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\big((\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)\big)\circ{{\mathcal{G}}}_{f}\Big(\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\big((\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)\big)\Big)=m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}\big((\pi\circ f,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)\big)
for all
pointed representations
(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}},\Omega)\in\mathbf{Rep}^{\bullet}({{\mathcal{A}}})_{0},

(f)

\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\circ{{\mathcal{G}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}=\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{S}}}({{\mathcal{A}}})},
and

(g)

m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}\big({{\mathcal{G}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}(\omega)\big)=\mathrm{id}_{{{\mathcal{G}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}(\omega)}

for all states
\omega\in{{\mathcal{S}}}({{\mathcal{A}}}).

The reader should compare this short definition to Constructions

3.1
,
3.17
, and

5.27
, which are
the usual definitions of the GNS construction.
Many of these properties are well-known, though less emphasis
is placed on allowing non-unitary intertwiners,
obfuscating the underlying categorical perspective outlined here.
We have established such a categorical framework
for the GNS constructing viewing it as a morphism/process
in an appropriate category, namely
\mathbf{Fun}(\mathbf{C^{*}\text{-}Alg}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathbf{Cat}),

and we have illustrated that the universal properties
of the GNS construction are encoded in this morphism being
left adjoint to the simple morphism/process that views normalized
vectors as states.

6.
Examples

The authors
of
[
9
]
include several examples, and we
will go through the simplest ones to illustrate the
meaning of our constructions and theorems.

Example 6.1
.

Let
{{\mathcal{A}}}:={{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathbb{C}}}^{2}),

2\times 2

matrices with complex coefficients.
This is the algebra of observables for a
spin-
\frac{1}{2}
system, i.e. a qubit.
Label an orthonormal basis by

\{\uparrow,\downarrow\}
—this basis refers to the
spin of a particle
along a particular axis.
Let
{{\mathcal{A}}}
act on
{{\mathbb{C}}}^{2}
by the identity
representation, meaning that the representation

\pi:{{\mathcal{A}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathbb{C}}}^{2})
is just the identity map.
Let
\omega_{\uparrow}:{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathbb{C}}}^{2})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathbb{C}}}
be the state
corresponding to a pure state with spin up, i.e.
26
26

26

We are using Dirac bra-ket notation
for the examples.

\omega_{\uparrow}(a):=\langle\uparrow\!|a|\!\uparrow\rangle
for all
a\in{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{H}}}).

Applying the restriction functor
\mathbf{rest}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}

to the pointed representation

\big(\pi,{{\mathbb{C}}}^{2},|\!\!\uparrow\rangle\big)
gives
\omega_{\uparrow}.

Next, apply the GNS construction
\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}

to the state
\omega_{\uparrow}.
As a vector space,

{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathbb{C}}}^{2})
is four-dimensional
with a basis given by

(6.2)

e_{\uparrow\uparrow}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&0\end{pmatrix},\quad e_{\uparrow\downarrow}=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
0&0\end{pmatrix},\quad e_{\downarrow\uparrow}=\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
1&0\end{pmatrix},\quad\&\quad e_{\downarrow\downarrow}=\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}.

The expectation values for these operators are
given by

(6.3)

\omega_{\uparrow}(e_{\uparrow\uparrow})=1,\quad\omega_{\uparrow}(e_{\uparrow\downarrow})=0,\quad\omega_{\uparrow}(e_{\downarrow\uparrow})=0,\quad\&\quad\omega_{\uparrow}(e_{\downarrow\downarrow})=0.

Notice that
27
27

27

To avoid confusion with
the physics literature,
for the purposes of this section,
we will use
†
to denote
the adjoint instead of
{}^{*}.

e_{\uparrow\downarrow}^{{\dagger}}e_{\uparrow\downarrow}=e_{\downarrow\downarrow}

and
e_{\downarrow\downarrow}^{{\dagger}}e_{\downarrow\downarrow}=e_{\downarrow\downarrow}
so that

\omega_{\uparrow}(e_{\uparrow\downarrow}^{{\dagger}}e_{\uparrow\downarrow})=0

and
\omega_{\uparrow}(e_{\downarrow\downarrow}^{{\dagger}}e_{\downarrow\downarrow})=0.

In fact,

(6.4)

\mathcal{N}_{\omega_{\uparrow}}=\mathrm{span}\{e_{\uparrow\downarrow},e_{\downarrow\downarrow}\}.

Then,
28
28

28

No completion is necessary here since the vector spaces
are finite-dimensional.

{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega_{\uparrow}}:={{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathbb{C}}}^{2})/\mathcal{N}_{\omega_{\uparrow}}

consists of equivalence classes of matrices

(6.5)

a=\begin{pmatrix}a_{\uparrow\uparrow}&a_{\uparrow\downarrow}\\
a_{\downarrow\uparrow}&a_{\downarrow\downarrow}\end{pmatrix},

where
a_{ij}\in{{\mathbb{C}}}
with
i,j\in\{\uparrow,\downarrow\},

and
a\sim b
if and only if

(6.6)

b-a=\begin{pmatrix}0&b_{\uparrow\downarrow}-a_{\uparrow\downarrow}\\
0&b_{\downarrow\downarrow}-a_{\downarrow\downarrow}\end{pmatrix}.

Note that the inner product on
{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega_{\uparrow}}
in this case is given by

(6.7)

{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega_{\uparrow}}\times{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega_{\uparrow}}\ni([a],[b])\mapsto\omega_{\uparrow}(a^{{\dagger}}b)=\overline{a_{\uparrow\uparrow}}b_{\uparrow\uparrow}+\overline{a_{\downarrow\uparrow}}b_{\downarrow\uparrow},

where the overline denotes complex conjugation.
The associated cyclic representation from the
GNS construction applied to the state
\omega_{\uparrow}
is

(\pi_{\omega_{\uparrow}},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega_{\uparrow}},[\mathds{1}]),

where
\mathds{1}
is the
2\times 2
identity
matrix and
\pi_{\omega_{\uparrow}}(a)\big([b]\big)=[ab]

is obtained from ordinary matrix multiplication.
The intertwiner
m_{{{\mathcal{A}}}}
from (
5.49
)
applied to our representation
\big(\pi,{{\mathbb{C}}}^{2},|\!\uparrow\rangle\big)

is the map
[a]\mapsto a|\!\!\uparrow\rangle.
Since our representation
was cyclic to begin with, this intertwiner is unitary. This
map compares our original Hilbert space representation
to the one obtained from the GNS construction in a
canonical way.

Example 6.8
.

Let
{{\mathcal{A}}}:={{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathbb{C}}}^{2})\otimes{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathbb{C}}}^{2})

and let
{{\mathcal{A}}}
act on
{{\mathbb{C}}}^{2}\otimes{{\mathbb{C}}}^{2}
via

(6.9)

\begin{split}{{\mathcal{A}}}&\xrightarrow{\pi}{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathbb{C}}}^{2}\otimes{{\mathbb{C}}}^{2})\\
a\otimes b&\mapsto\Big({{\mathbb{C}}}^{2}\otimes{{\mathbb{C}}}^{2}\ni|\psi\rangle\otimes|\phi\rangle\xmapsto{\pi(a\otimes b)}a|\psi\rangle\otimes b|\phi\rangle\Big)\end{split}

on elementary tensors and appropriately extended.
Let
\omega
be the state

(6.10)

{{\mathcal{A}}}\ni a\otimes b\mapsto\omega(a\otimes b):=\langle\Psi|\pi(a\otimes b)|\Psi\rangle

on
{{\mathcal{A}}}
obtained by pulling back
the vector state associated to the unit vector

(6.11)

|\Psi\rangle:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\Big(|\!\uparrow\downarrow\rangle-|\!\downarrow\uparrow\rangle\Big),

where
|\hskip-2.84526pt\uparrow\downarrow\rangle
is short for
|\hskip-2.84526pt\uparrow\rangle\otimes|\hskip-2.84526pt\downarrow\rangle.

Let the inner product on
{{\mathbb{C}}}^{2}\otimes{{\mathbb{C}}}^{2}

be the usual Euclidean inner product.
Using the same notation as in the previous example,

(6.12)

\begin{split}\omega(a\otimes b)&=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\langle\uparrow\downarrow\!|-\langle\downarrow\uparrow\!|\Big)\pi(a\otimes b)\Big(|\!\uparrow\downarrow\rangle-|\!\downarrow\uparrow\rangle\Big)\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\langle\uparrow\!|\otimes\langle\downarrow\!|-\langle\downarrow\!|\otimes\langle\uparrow\!|\Big)\Big(a|\!\uparrow\rangle\otimes b|\!\downarrow\rangle-a|\!\downarrow\rangle\otimes b|\!\uparrow\rangle\Big)\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\Big(a_{\uparrow\uparrow}b_{\downarrow\downarrow}-a_{\uparrow\downarrow}b_{\downarrow\uparrow}-a_{\downarrow\uparrow}b_{\uparrow\downarrow}+a_{\downarrow\downarrow}b_{\uparrow\uparrow}\Big).\end{split}

It is not necessary for us to calculate

{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega}
explicitly but we use the isomorphism
\pi
in (
6.9
)
to identify its elements as
equivalence classes
[a\otimes b]

of elements in
{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathbb{C}}}^{2})\otimes{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathbb{C}}}^{2}).

Because
|\Psi\rangle
is cyclic,
Lemma
5.57
shows that the map

(6.13)

\begin{split}\big(\pi_{\omega},{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega},[\mathds{1}]\big)&\xrightarrow{m_{{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathbb{C}}}^{2}\otimes{{\mathbb{C}}}^{2})}}\big(\pi,{{\mathbb{C}}}^{2}\otimes{{\mathbb{C}}}^{2},|\Psi\rangle\big)\\
[a\otimes b]&\xmapsto{\hskip 16.38895pt\hskip 8.19447pt\;\;\;}\pi(a\otimes b)|\Psi\rangle\end{split}

extended linearly is a unitary intertwiner.
Now, let
i_{1}:{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathbb{C}}}^{2})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathbb{C}}}^{2})\otimes{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathbb{C}}}^{2})

be the
C^{*}
-algebra map defined by

(6.14)

i_{1}(a):=a\otimes\mathds{1}.

Physically, such a map corresponds to an
observer
\mathcal{O}_{1}

only being able to make measurements
on the observables
{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathbb{C}}}^{2})

corresponding to a single particle. It is convenient
to denote the first
{{\mathbb{C}}}^{2}
by
{{\mathcal{H}}}_{1}

and the second by
{{\mathcal{H}}}_{2}.

This situation occurs, for instance,
in an EPR-like experiment,
where a particle decomposes into two particles whose spins
are correlated in a way described by the vector (
6.11
).
The two particles fly off in opposite directions and
observers far away are waiting to measure the spin.

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{1}}
\textstyle{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\Big(|\!\uparrow\downarrow\rangle-|\!\downarrow\uparrow\rangle\Big)}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{2}}

Observer
\mathcal{O}_{1}
cannot measure
the observables
{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{H}}}_{2})
and vice versa.
Therefore, the state that
\mathcal{O}_{1}

sees is given by the restriction

(6.15)

\omega_{1}:=\omega\circ i_{1}:{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{H}}}_{1})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{H}}}_{1})\otimes{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{H}}}_{2})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathbb{C}}}.

This state corresponds to the density matrix

(6.16)

\rho_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{2}&0\\
0&\frac{1}{2}\end{pmatrix}

on
{{\mathcal{H}}}_{1}
using our ordered basis

({|\hskip-2.84526pt\uparrow\rangle},{|\hskip-2.84526pt\downarrow\rangle}).

What is the GNS construction
applied to the state
\omega_{1}
and how is it related to
the original pointed representation

\big(\pi,{{\mathcal{H}}}_{1}\otimes{{\mathcal{H}}}_{2},|\Psi\rangle\big)
?
Let
a\in{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{H}}}_{1}).
Then

(6.17)

\big(a^{{\dagger}}a\big)_{ik}=\sum_{j\in\{\uparrow,\downarrow\}}(a^{{\dagger}})_{ij}a_{jk}=\sum_{j\in\{\uparrow,\downarrow\}}\overline{a_{ji}}a_{jk}

implies

(6.18)

\begin{split}\omega_{1}(a^{{\dagger}}a)&={\rm tr}(\rho_{1}a^{{\dagger}}a)=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\langle\uparrow\!|a^{{\dagger}}a|\!\uparrow\rangle+\langle\downarrow\!|a^{{\dagger}}a|\!\downarrow\rangle\Big)\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j\in\{\uparrow,\downarrow\}}\Big(|a_{j\uparrow}|^{2}+|a_{j\downarrow}|^{2}\Big)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j,k\in\{\uparrow,\downarrow\}}|a_{jk}|^{2}\end{split}

so that
\omega_{1}(a^{{\dagger}}a)=0

if and only if
a=0.
Therefore,
\mathcal{N}_{\omega_{1}}=\{0\}

and hence
{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega_{1}}={{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{H}}}_{1})
as a vector space.
The inner product on
{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega_{1}}
is given by

(6.19)

{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega_{1}}\times{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega_{1}}\ni(a,b)\mapsto\omega_{1}(a^{{\dagger}}b)=\frac{1}{2}{\rm tr}\left(a^{{\dagger}}b\right),

which is half the Hilbert-Schmidt (Frobenius) inner product.
Furthermore,
the associated GNS representation
\pi_{\omega_{1}}

acts as

(6.20)

\pi_{\omega_{1}}(a)b=ab=\sum_{i,j,k\in\{\uparrow,\downarrow\}}a_{ij}b_{jk}e_{ik},

where the
e_{ik}
are as in (
6.2
).
The induced map
L_{i_{1}}:{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega_{1}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega}

corresponding to
(
3.18
) is
given by

(6.21)

\begin{split}{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{H}}}_{1})\equiv{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega_{1}}&\xrightarrow{L_{i_{1}}=\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}_{i_{1}}(\omega)}{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega}\\
a&\xmapsto{\hskip 16.38895pt\hskip 16.38895pt\hskip 8.19447pt}[a\otimes\mathds{1}]\end{split}

Using this with the intertwiner

m_{{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{H}}}_{1}\otimes{{\mathcal{H}}}_{2})}

from (
6.13
),
gives a canonical intertwiner
of
{{\mathcal{B}}}({{\mathcal{H}}}_{1})
-representations
to our original Hilbert space

(6.22)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 116.4703pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-116.4703pt\raise 9.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.16666pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega_{1}}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-9.51889pt\raise 9.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.66666pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\omega}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 86.12495pt\raise 9.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 75.67361pt\raise-9.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 15.99155pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.49156pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{GNS}^{\bullet}_{i_{1}}(\omega)}$}}}\kern
